Macro trend Political cycle Ratings FC LC 
Activity + Presidential: 2026 S&P BBB- BBB- 
Fiscal Stimulative Parliamentary: 2026 Moody’s Ba2 Ba2 
Monetary Restrictive Local: 2028 Fitch BB+ BB+

Serbia’s economy has remained resilient despite elevated domestic 
and external uncertainty, supported by robust consumption, a 
strong labour market and continued investment ahead of EXPO 
2027. The more supportive fiscal stance, including higher social 
spending, should sustain near-term demand but could add to both 
inflation and external pressures. The unresolved NIS ownership issue 
remains the main energy risk, as the US Treasury’s extension lasts 
only until 30 October. Meanwhile, the 25 October parliamentary 
election and Aleksandar Vučić’s pursuit of the premiership add to 
the near-term political uncertainty, while Serbia’s long-term 
balancing of relations with the EU, Russia and China has become 
increasingly difficult against the current geopolitical backdrop. 
*Quarterly data is eop, annual is average; shading denotes ING forecast data 
Source: National sources, ING estimates

Real GDP (%YoY) and contributions (ppt)  Growth strengthens, but not without trade-offs

Economic activity strengthened during the first half of 2026, with 
real GDP growth accelerating to 3.8% year-on-year in the second 
quarter. Household consumption remained supported by rapid 
wage growth and a resilient labour market, while infrastructure 
investment linked to EXPO 2027 continued to underpin construction 
and domestic demand. Nevertheless, the increasingly import-
intensive investment cycle is weighing on the external balance, 
while foreign direct investment remains below its 2024 peak. 
Growth should remain relatively solid, although the outlook will 
depend on political normalisation, external demand and a durable 
resolution of the NIS refinery issue. 
Source: Eurostat, ING estimates

FX stability remains the main policy tool  FX stability to persist; rate cuts delayed

Inflation accelerated to 4.1% in September, returning close to the 
upper end of the NBS target band and highlighting continued 
uncertainty around food and energy prices. Against this backdrop, 
the central bank kept the key rate unchanged at 5.75% in October, 
making near-term monetary easing less likely. Meanwhile, EUR/RSD 
has remained broadly stable within the 117.3–117.5 range, 
supported by official interventions. The NBS sold €475m between 
January and September to maintain currency stability. We expect 
the NBS to maintain a firm grip on the dinar going forwards. 
Source: NBS, ING estimates

SERBIA USD credit spreads (bp)  Sovereign credit: Stable fundamentals vs political noise

Despite some deterioration in recent years (wider fiscal deficit, drop 
off in F  ), Serbia’s fundamentals remain closer to higher-rated IG 
peers, and generally even stronger than most in the CEE region. The 
focus remains on pre-election fiscal loosening and energy pressures 
on the external balance. However, politics remains the point of 
uncertainty, with upcoming elections bringing, at a minimum, the 
potential for further anti-government protests and greater 
uncertainty about the outcome than at any time in the past 
decade. We would therefore maintain a favourable fundamental 
view on the sovereign, but reduce some exposure ahead of 
elections.  
Source: Refinitiv, ING   James Wilson, EM Sovereign Strategist

Macro trend Political cycle Ratings FC LC 
Activity – Presidential: May 2028 S&P BB- BB- 
Fiscal Tight Parliamentary: May 2028 Moody’s Ba3 Ba3

The Middle East remains focused on the US-Iran standoff. In this 
environment, pressure on energy prices persists, creating challenges 
for net energy-importing economies such as Turkey. Domestically, 
the closure of certain investment funds prompted some investors to 
unwind money market fund positions, although dollarization 
pressures remained contained. CBT reserves declined in September 
amid weaker confidence, while maintaining strong reserve buffers 
remains important for absorbing potential shocks and preserving 
the exchange rate as a key policy anchor. Slower economic activity, 
softer domestic demand, low core goods inflation and easing 
services inflation have reduced upside inflation risks, leading the CBT 
to lower the effective funding cost. Markets continue to expect 
further rate cuts this year, although reserve volatility and investor 
confidence remain important considerations for the CBT. *Quarterly data is eop, annual is average Source: National sources, ING estimates

GDP growth (% YoY)  Macro digest

Elevated borrowing costs and macroprudential measures, which 
were tightened further in May, appear to be contributing to a more 
pronounced slowdown in second-quarter 2026 GDP growth. 
Domestic demand, particularly private consumption, has continued 
to lose momentum, making its weakest contribution since 2024 as 
spending adjusts to tighter financial conditions. At the same time, 
net exports have returned to positive territory and are providing 
support to overall economic activity. On a sequential basis, growth 
was held back by weaker private and government consumption, 
although this was largely offset by a strong contribution from net 
exports. The economy is on a moderating growth path in the second 
half of this year; however, the recent disruption in investment funds 
is likely to place additional pressure on domestic demand, 
increasing downside risks to our full-year GDP growth forecast of 
2.8%. Compared with the previous plan, the new 2027-2029 
Medium-Term Plan (MTP) projects a growth composition with a 
larger contribution from private consumption, a significantly higher 
inflation trajectory, stronger primary spending and a wider current 
account deficit next year. These projections come ahead of 
elections that, according to signals from the government bloc, are 
likely to be held in early 2028. Against this backdrop, GDP growth is 
expected to accelerate to above 4% next year. A potential 
resolution of the Gulf conflict could support growth prospects, while 
any further escalation would add to already high downside risks.  
Lower-than-expected September CPI contributed to a further 
decline in annual inflation. Given still high energy prices and 
unanchored inflation expectations, we see end-2026 inflation at 
29.0% though risks are on the downside with weakening demand. 
The latest MTP revised the 2027 inflation forecast upwards to 21%, 
compared with 9% in the previous plan. The new projection is 
broadly in line with the 21.9% consensus in the Market Participants 
Survey. The upwards revision to the 2027 forecast was driven partly 
by the carry-over impact of higher-than-expected inflation this year 
on the following year’s starting point, and partly by the global 
economic backdrop. According to CBT calculations, the impact of the 
US-Iran war and related global developments on 2026 inflation is 
estimated at around 7ppt. After eliminating war related factors, 
inflation would be below 25%. The new 2027 inflation forecast 
indicates that the disinflation process has been pushed back further 
in the current stabilisation programme, in place since mid-2023. 
Uncertainty surrounding oil prices - along with their spillover effects 
on other commodity prices - continues to pose risks to the inflation 
outlook as well as to the expected fiscal impulse envisaged in the 
MTP. Accordingly, we forecast inflation next year at 25%, with risks 
on the upside.   
Source: TurkStat, ING

### Page 57

Directional Economics CEEMEA – Central Europe’s new horizons: from investment hosts to global investors October 2026

57 
Turkey 16Bmuhammet.mercan@ing.com.tr

Unemployment vs NPLs (%)  Labour market stability conceals rising pressures

The seasonally adjusted unemployment rate has remained broadly 
stable in the 8-9% range since mid-2024, occasionally falling below 
8% and reaching 7.8% in August 2026. As job creation has been 
largely flat, the relatively low unemployment rate mainly reflects a 
gradual decline in labour force participation from nearly 54% to 
52.5%. Broader labour market indicators suggest increasing strain, 
with the underutilisation rate, which includes the unemployed, the 
potential labour force and time-related underemployment, rising to 
31.0%, close to an historic peak. Meanwhile, asset quality is 
gradually weakening, largely due to consumer lending. Although 
the overall NPL ratio remains historically low, rising GPL ratios 
warrant caution and may require further restructurings, potentially 
accelerated by recent fund-related stress. 
Source: TurkStat, BRSA, ING

Breakdown of C/A financing (12m-rolling, US$bn)  External risks are still alive

The current account deficit has been on a widening trend, while 
capital flows have remained volatile recently. The trajectory of the 
current account balance is expected to be influenced by a mix of 
external risks alongside domestic demand conditions. In this regard, 
geopolitical shocks increase upside pressures on the deficit given 
the elevated trajectory of oil and gas prices. The signing of an 
agreement between the US and Iran, on the other hand, should be 
supportive with easing pressure on energy prices. The government 
forecasts a current account deficit of 2.6% of GDP in 2026, followed 
by a gradual improvement in subsequent years. While the new 
projections are higher than those in the previous plan, the elevated 
and gradually increasing growth path outlined in the MTP raises 
doubts over whether a narrowing of the current account deficit can 
be achieved. Source: CBT, ING

Primary balance (12m-rolling, % of GDP)  Fiscal outlook faces energy-related pressures

The budget deficit has remained below the government’s target. 
However, the fiscal burden from energy subsidies is expected to 
continue in the near term as elevated global energy prices remain a 
challenge. We forecast a 2026 budget deficit of 3.3% of GDP, with 
lower capital expenditure and resilient tax revenue partly offsetting 
subsidy costs. The MTP projects a central government deficit of 3.1% 
of GDP, rising to 3.5% in 2027 before declining to 2.8% by 2029. 
Although most earthquake-related reconstruction spending has 
been completed, total spending is expected to rise from 23.0% to 
24.5% of GDP, mainly due to higher current transfers, reserve 
allocations and interest payments. Stronger revenue collection, 
equating to around 1% of GDP, should provide a partial offset, while 
fiscal policy is intended to support disinflation. Source: Ministry of Treasury and Finance, ING

Banking sector volume expansion  CBT’s targeted support for liquidity and  MEs

The CBT has introduced targeted measures to support market liquidity 
and SME lending. The SME loan growth limit was increased from 
4.5% to 5.0%, reversing the tightening implemented on 23 May. The 
Bank also lowered the share of TRY required reserves held in blocked 
accounts, giving banks greater flexibility in liquidity management 
without changing overall reserve requirements. These steps are 
designed to address temporary liquidity and credit pressures rather 
than signal a broader easing of monetary policy. Meanwhile, loan 
growth has slowed due to the macroprudential measures 
introduced in May and the continued tight policy stance. TRY-
denominated loans remain the main driver of commercial lending 
because of stricter FX lending limits, while weaker momentum in 
TRY SME lending has widened the gap with non-SME lending. Source: BRSA, ING

Turkey Strategy

FX: Spot vs forward and INGF  FX strategy (with Frantisek Taborsky, EMEA FX & FI Strategist)

Fund volatility has had little impact on the dollarization ratio for 
deposits and mutual funds. TRY funds recorded outflows of around 
TRY580bn between end-August and 14 October as some investors 
seemed to be unwinding their money market fund holdings (the 
amount excludes price effects and funds for which trading was 
suspended, though it has been hard to fully track activity since 
15 September as some funds shifted to monthly valuations). On the 
other hand, we estimate that outflows in FX- and gold-linked assets 
held in funds stood at US$2.8bn. Recent increase in TRY deposits and 
repos suggests that dollarization remains contained.  
Following more than US$30bn net FX purchases between June and 
August, the CBT’s net FX sales came in at US$8.5bn between 
1 September and 9 October. We see US$2.0bn net portfolio 
outflows, US$3.0bn swap outflows, US$3.1bn increase in residents’ 
FX-linked assets (netted across banks and funds), and a US$1.1bn 
increase in residents’ gold deposits (parity-adjusted), which leaves 
us US$4.3bn to account for. Hence, we think roughly half of the net 
FX sales were related to residents’ FX demand in this period with 
corporates having a higher weight. While the CBT recently adjusted 
the pace of FX depreciation higher, it is likely a mechanical move 
rather than a shift in FX policy.  
We continue to favour TRY for carry trades, anticipating that the 
carry return will continue to outweigh depreciation.  
Source: Bloomberg, ING estimates  
CBT funding

Source: CBT, ING

Yield curve (%)  Fixed income strategy (with Frantisek Taborsky, EMEA FX & FI

Strategist; James Wilson, EM Sovereign Strategist) 
Given the September CPI outcome and weakness in domestic 
demand, we see the policy rate at 35% by the end of 2026 following 
two 100bp cuts, at the October and December MPC meetings. The 
CBT also started purchase auctions on 20 August and, by mid-
October, had purchased TRY117.8bn (nominal). According to the 
Bank, MMFs hold a significant amount of TLREF-linked instruments, 
which has led them to accelerate bond purchases. Continuation of 
purchases should also be supportive for bond yields. 
The market is pricing in a CBT rate path for this year that is nearly 
identical to ours but remains significantly more hawkish regarding 
next year, pricing in a final rate cut that would subsequently be 
reversed, leaving the rate unchanged. With growing conviction that 
the disinflationary process will continue, we see room for further 
curve steepening and lower front-end, while the long end remains 
elevated amid global uncertainty. 
In the Eurobond space, Turkey has held up well despite the global 
macro volatility in recent months and local investment fund noise. 
We continue to see fundamentals as relatively stable against a 
backdrop of higher energy prices and global bond yields. In this 
context, a decent pickup of 30-40bp over the BB sovereign average 
remains attractive, despite some tightening in recent weeks.  
Source: Bloomberg, ING estimates

Source: Treasury, CBT, ING

The hryvnia remained under pressure as the dollar strengthened, 
partly due to renewed Middle East tensions. Consequently, USD/UAH 
temporarily broke above 45.0, reaching its highest level on record. 
Hryvnia depreciation could have been sharper, but NBU actions 
provided support. In September, the central bank raised rates by 
50bp to 16.0% and maintained sizeable FX interventions, with the 
four-week average close to record highs, which demonstrates the 
NBU’s determination to stabilise the exchange rate. FX reserves 
remain volatile but new tranches of foreign loans should lift them to 
around US$60bn. The macroeconomic environment remains 
challenging due to the ongoing war with Russia, despite intensified 
rhetoric and pressure from the US administration. 
*Quarterly data is eop, annual is average. Shading denotes ING forecast data 
Source: National sources, ING estimates

GDP growth (%)  Economic stagnation in 2026 due to war disruptions

n 2026, Ukraine’s G P level seems set to remain 20% lower than in 
2021, prior to Russia’s full-scale invasion in February 2022. The 
economy has nevertheless shown considerable resilience during the 
war. However, the intensification of attacks on energy, warehousing 
and logistics infrastructure has led to severe supply-side disruptions 
compounded by labour shortages, weighing on GDP growth, which 
slowed to 1.8% in 2025 and is expected to fall below 1% in 2026. 
The recent acceleration in inflation has added to these pressures by 
eroding real incomes and private consumption.  
Assuming hostilities continue into 2027, economic activity is likely to 
rebound only gradually with energy shortages posing an additional 
risk during the winter months. The geopolitical outlook remains 
highly uncertain with little prospect of an imminent end to the war 
despite intensified diplomatic efforts. 
Source: NSI, ING

Inflation and NBU policy rate (%)  Return of double-digit inflation may require NBU rate hikes

Price pressures have increased in recent months as a consequence 
of intensified Russian attacks on energy, logistics and civil 
infrastructure and the war in the Middle East (global oil shock). CPI 
inflation accelerated from 8.1% YoY in August to 10.0% YoY in 
September, well above expectations. Monthly inflation of 2.1% MoM 
was the highest since 2015. The surge in inflation was broad-based, 
and included not only energy and food categories, but also core 
inflation, which reached 8.8% YoY in September, up from 8.1% YoY 
in August. Higher food prices surprised despite the relatively robust 
harvest of grains, fruits and vegetables. War-related disruptions and 
unfavourable global trends may further fuel inflationary pressures 
and require additional NBU policy tightening in 2026 and a 
restrictive policy stance in 2027 to contain second-round effects. 
Source: NBU, ING

Fiscal and external current account balances (% of GDP)  Persistent fiscal gap funded mainly by EU loans in 2026-27

To finance the war with Russia, Ukraine has mobilised substantial 
internal resources while relying heavily on external financial 
assistance in the form of foreign loans from international partners. 
This includes the €90bn EU loan due to be disbursed in 2026-27, 
which comprises both defence and budget-support components. 
This has translated into fast rising public and external debt levels, 
both of which exceeded 100% of GDP in 2025, and are set to rise 
further in the coming years.  
Both fiscal and external current account gaps, as well as public and 
external debt levels, are set to widen significantly in the coming 
years. In July, the NBU projected foreign assistance inflows at 
US$87bn in 2026, US$59 in 2027, and US$33bn in 2028. Source: Ministry of Finance, NBU, IMF, WB, ING

Uzbekistan remains our preferred story in the CCA region, combining 
strong and diversified growth, continued reforms and positive 
sovereign-credit momentum. An expected catch-up in gold exports, 
alongside ongoing capital inflows linked to the privatisation pipeline 
(UzNIF+12 SOEs) is supporting a positive performance from the 
soum. Combined with fiscal consolidation, this has helped bring the 
inflation rate down since the start of the year, improving the scope 
for further monetary easing. At the same time, longer-term 
vulnerabilities stem from persisting twin deficits, continued high 
state involvement in the economy and a long history of above-
target inflation. A more meaningful monetary easing and a lasting 
break from UZS’s depreciation trend would require a further decline 
in inflation and more durable private capital inflows. 
*Quarterly data is eop, annual is average. Shading denotes ING forecast data 
Source: National sources, ING estimates

GDP growth by sectors (% YoY, ppt)  Activity: Accelerating beyond potential

Uzbekistan’s G P growth has been exceptionally strong recently, 
supported by domestic demand catching up from a low base. While 
activity is expected to moderate from the current above-potential 
levels, Uzbekistan should remain one of the fastest-growing 
economies in emerging markets. Strong investment spending, 
industrial activity and ongoing reforms continue to support 
expansion, while positive demographic trends provide an additional 
tailwind. A gradual slowdown in the GDP growth is likely to reflect 
normalisation in the activity trends, with growth remaining 
comfortably above regional peers. Uzbekistan's challenge is 
increasingly one of managing overheating pressures rather than 
finding additional sources growth in consumption and investment. 
Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Inflation, key rate, and CPI expectations (%)  Monetary policy: Scope for gradual easing

Over the past couple of years, inflation in Uzbekistan has moderated 
substantially from previous highs, aided by the recent fiscal 
consolidation, and this has created room for a cautious monetary 
policy easing cycle. However, current CPI remains above the central 
bank's long-term objective, while strong domestic demand, 
administrative tariff adjustments and global food and commodity 
risks argue against aggressive rate cuts. We therefore expect the 
central bank to deliver only gradual easing from a still-restrictive 
level. Real rates remain relatively attractive and should continue to 
support macro stability while allowing policymakers to balance 
growth objectives against the need for further disinflation. Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  
/ 
Balance of payments composition (US$bn)  External balance: Gold and capital inflows support UZS

Our stronger-than-consensus medium-term view on the soum 
continues to be reinforced by recent developments. High real rates, 
gold prices, an expected catch-up in physical gold exports and 
growing foreign participation in Uzbekistan's privatisation 
programme provide important support for the balance of payments 
and FX market. This should underpin the resilience of the UZS 
despite the country's persistent trade and current-account deficits. 
However, the longer-term outlook is less certain, as Uzbekistan 
continues to run sizeable twin deficits, while inflation remains above 
target. As a result, we view the UZS positively in the near term but 
believe a durable break from the historical depreciation trend would 
require sustained progress in reducing macro imbalances and 
deepening structural reforms. 
Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Document 305: France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

2027 Budget: Fiscal consolidation must now resume

The 2027 budget, adopted by the French Council of Ministers today, calls for a reduction in France’s fiscal 
deficit to 5% of GDP in 2027 (down from 5.4% in 2026). This is a first step toward s the multi-year fiscal 
consolidation needed to stabilise the public debt-to-GDP ratio. This ratio is expected to rise further in 2027 
and until the deficit is reduced to 3% of GDP. Furthermore, given the increase in some expenditures (+0.6 pp 
of GDP for debt service and defence spending), the projected fiscal effort amounts to 1 pp. Nearly one-third 
of this effort would come from higher compulsory levies, and two -thirds from curbing government and 
social spending.

2027 Draft finance law: A step in the right direction. The government is forecasting an improvement in the fiscal 
deficit from 5.4% of GDP in 2026 to 5% in 2027. This projection is based on a credible growth assumption of 1% 
(see our analysis of French growth drivers). To achieve this target, compulsory levies (CL) are expected to rise to 
44.2% of GDP, an increase of 0.3 pp (the same increase as in 2026, compared with +0.9 pp in 2025). The public 
spending-to-GDP ratio would decline by 0.2 pp to 56.9%. The effort required to reduce the deficit to 5% is greater 
than it appears. Indeed, some expenditures will increase (interest payments by 0.4 pp and defence spending by 
0.2 pp). Consequently, the fiscal effort for next year amounts not to 0.4 pp, but to 1 pp of GDP. One-third of this will 
be achieved through increased CL, and two-thirds through spending cuts. Social spending will contribute, notably 
through the cancellation of EUR 3 billion in social contribution reductions for low-wage earners, as well as efforts 
totaling EUR 6 billion each in the areas of pensions and healthcare. Social spending shows the largest deviation 
from pre-COVID levels, with an increase representing 1.2 pp of GDP, nearly half of the gap between the 2026 
budget balance and a 3% deficit. Government spending, excluding interest payments and defence, is expected to 
be frozen in nominal terms.

A compromise seems likely despite a tight schedule. The French National Assembly will start reviewing the 
budget on Tuesday 13 October. The government will need to ensure that a majority vote against the draft budget 
does not form. The current circumstances, including increasing sovereign bond yields and the upcoming 
presidential election, call for a consensus based on fiscal consolidation of the size proposed by the government. If 
Parliament does not pass a budget by the end of the year, the government will have to choose between 
implementing the government’s budget proposal by executive order ( only possible if there was no parliamentary 
vote) or through a special law (as in 2025 and 2026). A special law would enable tax collection , while the 
government and social security agencies could continue to issue debt. In this case, the 2026 budget would apply 
in 2027 (without indexation) until Parliament passes a budget in the first few weeks of the year. This must occur 
before the end of February (when Parliament’s session ends, earlier than usual due to the presidential election).

The one-year postponement of the 5% budget deficit target has a measurable impact. The gap between the 
budget adopted for 2026 (deficit of 5% of GDP) and the actual budget outcome (deficit of 5.4%) is due to 
unforeseeable circumstances (the conflict in Iran  and extreme drought). However, postponing the deficit target  
previously set for 2026 to 2027 comes at a cost: 1) the public debt-to-GDP ratio is expected to reach 121% in 2027 
according to our forecasts (1 pp above our scenario with a 5% target in 2026 and a deficit reduced to 4.5% in 2027), 
and 2) the (upcoming) postponement to 2032 of the 3% of GDP deficit target (a commitment that most presidential 
candidates plan to make) rather than in 2029 (the government had committed to this with the European Commission 
in 2024). In 2032, the public debt-to-GDP ratio would stabilise at 124% of GDP (instead of 121.5% if the deficit had 
been reduced to 5% of GDP as early as 2026 and to 3% of GDP by the end of the decade).

Another delay in fiscal consolidation would further erode France’s credibility. This new delay could stem 
either from a decision by the government formed in May 2027 to immediately implement part of its agenda, or from 
the enactment of a special law that would remain in effect until (just after) the likely legislative elections in June  
2027  or  for  the  entire  year.  If  the  government  emerging  from  the  elections  were  to  resume  fiscal  
consolidation only in 2028 (with a deficit of 5% of GDP that it projects to reduce to 3% by 2032), public debt would 
reach 126% of GDP by that date (a one-year delay in fiscal consolidation would result in an additional 2 pp of 
debt). This further delay could potentially undermine the credibility of the next government, given that budget 
targets were  not met in three out of four years between 2023 and 2026, a situation that would be costly amid a 
sharp rise in long-term interest rates. Furthermore, a higher deficit would increase the amount of debt to be issued 
within an already  tight schedule. The Agence France Trésor (AFT) has announced debt issuances totaling EUR 
340 billion in 2027 (an  increase  of  EUR  20  billion  compared  to  2026).  This  increase  is  due  to  the  maturing  
COVID  related  debt (beginning in 2027 and continuing largely through 2030), while the amount of debt to be 
issued to finance the deficit will remain stable compared to 2026. A decrease from this last driver would be needed 
in subsequent years so that  the AFT’s debt issuance can stabilise.

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of the Advanced Economies Team, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

### Page 1

Oil rallied yesterday as markets grow increasingly concerned
about the risk of a renewed escalation in tensions between the
US and Iran

Warren Patterson and Ewa Manthey
Energy – European gas storage draws start
Oil prices surged yesterday, with front-month ICE Brent settling 4%
higher at just above $113/bbl. The rally is being driven less by
current fundamentals than by mounting supply concerns. Markets
are increasingly worried about renewed escalation in the Persian
Gulf, a scenario that could threaten oil ﬂows that have so far
remained remarkably resilient.
Reports suggest President Trump is considering fresh strikes on Iran,
while the US is reinforcing its military presence in the region with the
deployment of a second aircraft carrier. Meanwhile, there are
reports that US troops have been evacuated from some bases in
the Middle East. Clearly, these developments don’t paint an
optimistic picture. They call into question our long-held view that
the US midterm elections would bring de-escalation and

normalisation in ﬂows and oil prices. If anything, re-escalation
seems the most likely path. As a result, our expectation that ICE
Brent would average $80/bbl over 4Q26 is looking unattainable.
Even if tensions don't re-escalate, the mere possibility is likely to
keep a sizeable geopolitical risk premium embedded in prices.
European gas prices also remain extremely sensitive to

developments in the Persian Gulf, with LNG ﬂows increasingly likely
to be largely absent from the market for at least much of the
Northern Hemisphere winter. TTF rallied 3% yesterday, settling

storage also appears to have peaked at just under 75% full, with
storage falling marginally on Tuesday.
Metals — Gold falls as energy risks reignite
inﬂation concerns
Gold prices failed to beneﬁt from yesterday's equity sell-oﬀ and the

signiﬁcant fall in US Treasury yields, with the 10-year yield falling
back below 5%. Instead, gold prices came under pressure. Spot
prices fell 2.8% and are nearing $4,000/oz. The gold market
appears more focused on higher oil prices and what it could mean
for inﬂation — and ultimately central bank policy. With US inﬂation
still above the central bank’s 2% target, higher energy prices could
intensify price pressures and strengthen the case for tighter

monetary policy.
LME copper prices declined for a second consecutive session, falling
more than 1.9% yesterday, as rising exchange inventories and a
selloﬀ in AI-related stocks weighed on broader risk sentiment. LME
data showed copper inventories increased by 27,675 tonnes for a
fourth consecutive session to 272,950 tonnes as of yesterday, the
highest level since 27 July 2026.

Document 375: The Dutch economy is doing surprisingly well

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Dutch economy is doing surprisingly well.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Dutch economy continues to grow at a decent pace
despite persistent inﬂation, geopolitical uncertainty and
domestic capacity constraints. Strong household spending has
helped so far, but the growth engine is gradually shifting
towards exports and investment
Marcel Klok
We expect Dutch GDP growth of 1.7% in 2026, a solid outcome given the
challenging external backdrop
It has been a turbulent year for the global economy, but economic
activity has proven remarkably resilient. The Netherlands is no

exception. Dutch GDP grew by 0.6% quarter-on-quarter in the
second quarter, with household consumption and exports
performing particularly well.
We expect GDP growth of 1.7% in 2026 and 1.6% in 2027, following
growth of 1.6% in 2025. That is a decent performance given the
external environment. Energy prices remain elevated, interest rates
have risen again, and uncertainty surrounding the Middle East and
Russia continues to aﬀect businesses and consumers. But the

Cookie settings

Conﬁrm selection Accept all

### Page 2

enormous investment boom around AI and semiconductors has
helped world trade to continue to expand.
Global trade growth is also a Dutch growth
story
The strong performance of exports illustrates this particularly well.
Dutch export growth is expected to reach 3.4% this year. Export
performance in the second quarter was the strongest in eight
quarters and, importantly, the improvement was fairly broad-
based.
Services exports have been strong, while machinery and equipment

shipments, exports of oil products, and trade in energy-intensive
intermediate goods also performed well.
Consumers continue to spend despite low
conﬁdence
Domestic demand has also performed better than sentiment
indicators would suggest. Household consumption was the largest
contributor to GDP growth in the second quarter, while the
household savings rate declined slightly. This was despite
consumer conﬁdence remaining low, albeit at a somewhat higher

level. Wage growth is still stronger than inﬂation, supporting real
disposable income and allowing households to increase spending.
There are limits to how long this support can last, however. Inﬂation
remains stubbornly high. We expect HICP inﬂation of 2.8% this year
and 2.9% in 2027, with inﬂation likely to peak in the ﬁrst half of
next year as previous energy price increases work their way
through food and other consumer prices. Lower oil and fuel prices

should subsequently provide some relief, but developments remain
highly dependent on geopolitics and energy markets.
As wage growth gradually slows, real-income growth will lose
momentum. For a large share of households, purchasing-power
gains in 2027 are likely to be close to zero as wage growth
decelerates, taxes increase, and the temporary boost to pension
incomes fades. Still, there is some room for consumption growth if

### Page 3

households become more willing to spend and reduce discretionary
saving somewhat.
Investment is ﬁnally showing some signs of
life
Business investment has been one of the weak spots of the Dutch
economy for quite some time, but there are tentative signs that the
picture is improving. Investment increased for a second consecutive
quarter in the second quarter of the year, including higher
expenditure on transport and telecom equipment. We expect
investment growth to strengthen further in 2027.

That should not be confused with a genuine investment boom.
Businesses still face some very Dutch obstacles. Electricity-grid
congestion, nitrogen-emission restrictions and slow permitting
procedures continue to constrain investment and productive
capacity. Even where government policy eventually eases these
constraints, the impact will take time.
Public investment provides another source of demand.
Infrastructure, defence and electricity-grid spending will increase,

partly compensating for weaker growth in other areas of
government expenditure.
The labour market is cooling, but only
gently
The labour market is moving towards a slightly looser balance.
Unemployment is expected to edge up from 4.0% in 2026 to 4.1%
next year. Previous high increases in labour costs have coincided
with weaker employment growth, but shortages of workers remain
an important constraint for many companies.
This gradual cooling is actually positive from an inﬂation

perspective, helping to ease wage pressures without signalling a
sharp weakening in employment. For now, the labour market
appears to be normalising rather than heading towards a
downturn.

### Page 4

Budget negotiations add another layer of
uncertainty
Meanwhile, the process of securing parliamentary approval for the
Dutch central-government budget for 2027 remains unusually
uncertain. On Budget Day (Prinsjesdag) on 15 September, the
minority government formally submitted the 2027 Budget
Memorandum (Miljoenennota), the departmental budgets, and the
tax plan. Politically, however, these remain an opening position: the
government has no majority in the House of Representatives

(Tweede Kamer) nor in the Senate (Eerste Kamer) and must
negotiate with opposition parties to pass its plans.
The government has already revised parts of the package in
response to Parliament’s wishes. Most importantly, it withdrew the
proposed changes to unemployment and disability beneﬁts from
the Social Aﬀairs budget to give employers and trade unions room
to negotiate a broader agreement on social security and the labour
market. The associated austerity target has not disappeared,

however. It has provisionally been placed elsewhere in the
expenditure framework in the form of incomplete inﬂation
compensation for most ministerial departments. If negotiations do
not produce suﬃcient savings, the government intends to decide in
spring 2027 how the shortfall should actually be covered.
The Dutch government's Box 3 wealth tax
The part of Dutch wealth income tax that covers savings and
investments but excludes owner-occupied homes and pensions

remains an important unresolved tax issue. The government has
chosen a broad direction that seems to have support in parliament:
from 2028, it wants capital gains on most ﬁnancial investments to
be taxed when they are realised rather than taxing annual
increases in value. This requires an amending bill to the Box 3
legislation already before Parliament. Yet the ﬁnancing of this
change remains disputed.
Parliament rejected the government’s initial approach, which would

have brought substantially more households into Box 3 and would
not have covered the revenue losses in the more distant future.

### Page 5

Discussions on alternative funding remain ongoing and continue to
cast a shadow over departmental budgets, which are being
debated and voted on separately. The clock is ticking. Tax measures
intended to take eﬀect on 1 January 2027 must still be approved
by both chambers and published before the end of the year if they
are to enter into force as planned, as these require enacted and
published legislation. This is diﬀerent for the departmental budgets

that set spending targets, as without agreement existing policies
may continue, albeit with restraint.
For the economic outlook, this means that the precise composition
and timing of ﬁscal policy in 2027 remain unusually uncertain,
even though a substantial part of government spending will
continue irrespective of the political negotiations.
Resilient growth, but becoming somewhat
more export and investment-driven
All in all, the Dutch economy enters the ﬁnal part of 2026 in

reasonably good shape. Growth remains broad enough to
withstand weaker purchasing-power developments, while the
gradual recovery in investment and strong export demand provide
support as household and government consumption become less
powerful growth engines.
The downside risks are still dominated by the international
environment. A renewed escalation in the Middle East could push
oil, fuel and especially European gas prices higher, prolonging
inﬂation and weakening household purchasing power and business

investment. Geopolitical fragmentation and, in particular,
additional restrictions on semiconductor machinery trade could
also become increasingly consequential now. But the global
semiconductor and AI investment boom could also remain stronger
for longer.
Another upside could come from households: consumption has
already held up better than conﬁdence indicators suggested, and a
further decline in precautionary saving would allow spending to
grow faster even if real-income gains become more modest.

Document 403: US MARKET CALL_ Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKET CALL_ Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail
Date:    3 hours ago
US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is
Too Big To Fail
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 17 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Nils Rasmusson / Unsplash
I. Bonds
We've all been suffering from AI fatigue for a while. The daily news about it
has been head-spinning. We've been warned that AI is a bubble that will
burst, just as the late-1990s tech bubble led to the Tech Wreck in the early
2000s. Analogies to the credit excesses of the Great Financial Crisis have

### Page 3

also been proffered. Last week's rout in AI-related stocks convinced the
naysayers that the AI bubble is bursting, just as they’ve long predicted it
would.
Our happier spin is that the Bond Vigilantes are now doing a good job of
regulating and allocating capital to the AI ecosystem. Credit-quality
spreads are widening to reflect the riskiness of the bonds issued by some
of the dodgier AI-related borrowers like CoreWeave and Oracle. The
rapidly rising cost of credit for such borrowers should deflate the AI bubble

without bursting it. If some AI-related credits do blow up, the Godfathers of
the AI mafia (along with their protectors in the government) will clean up
the mess. As in "The Godfather II," Michael Corleone has too much at
stake to let his brother Fredo screw up the family business.
Meanwhile, the rout in the AI corporate bond market has triggered a flight
to quality and safety that has stopped the rout in the US Treasury bond
market (chart). This confirms our thesis that the recent rise in US bond

yields mostly reflects better-than-expected economic growth. Tougher
credit conditions in the AI corporate bond market should curb some of the
AI excesses. However, we doubt this will trigger an economy-wide credit
crunch or a recession. We aren't convinced that it will slow capital
spending much, since labor and materials shortages, along with public
NIMBY pushback, have already slowed the pace of data center
construction.
II. S&P Earnings

### Page 4

The big banks reported mixed but solid overall Q3 earnings last week. On
average, they met banking industry analysts' expectations. Last week, the
consensus EPS for all analysts covering S&P 500 companies edged up to
$365.70 this year, $424.19 next year, and $492.62 in 2028 (chart). Those
are extremely bullish expectations considering that we are still forecasting
$500 in 2030! If the analysts are right, the S&P 500 could rise to 10,000
by the end of next year, assuming a forward P/E of 20.0-21.0. For now, we

are sticking with 10,000 by the end of the Roaring 2020s, i.e., at the end
of 2029.
Last week, Q3's consensus EPS growth rate remained at 24.0% y/y, while
the Q4 estimate rose to 29.8% (chart). We've been among the most
bullish investment strategists on the Street, but analysts are still ahead of
us.

### Page 5

The S&P 500, S&P 400, and S&P 600 continued to rise to record highs
last week (chart).
III. Stock Valuation
The recent stock market selloff has been driven by falling forward P/Es
even as forward earnings have been rising to record highs (chart). The
former are nearing the troughs they made during last year's "Liberation
Day" lows.
Since roughly mid-2025, rising FEMO (fabulous earnings momentum) has
more than offset falling FOMO (fear of missing out) (chart). Since October

6, the reverse has been true: Falling FOMO is outpacing the rise in
FEMO.

### Page 6

IV. Performance
The stock market had a tough week last week. Leading the 1.95%
pullback was MAGS (down 3.89%), while XMAGS fell 1.38% (chart). The
Russell 2000 fell 2.4%, while the Nasdaq fell 4.5%. The DJIA rose 0.67%
for the week. The Impressive 493 is still outperforming the Magnificent-7
on a year-to-date basis, with a gain of 14.1% versus 10.2%.
V. Sentiment
The market letter writers have been more bullish than retail investors
lately (chart). Odds are they will both be a bit more pessimistic this week.

### Page 7

Only 47.4% of the S&P 500 are trading above their 200-day moving
averages (chart).
VI. Commodities
Aluminum prices are falling after matching their 2022 peak. This suggests
that supply-chain disruptions attributable to Gulf War III are receding, as
they had done after Russia invaded Ukraine (chart).

### Page 8

Copper prices remained near their recent record high last week despite
chatter that tightening credit conditions could slow the AI buildout and
weigh on overall GDP growth (chart).
The outlook for higher-for-longer diesel prices offers no relief (chart).

### Page 9

We aren't giving up on gold yet. The current correction resembles
previous ones that occurred in the middle of significant gold bull runs
(chart). This time, rising interest rates have been weighing on the metal's
price. However, significant buying by the People's Bank of China and
other central banks isn't interest-rate sensitive. We are still aiming for a
gold price of $5,000 an ounce by the end of this year or early next year.

featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

Document 529: 伊朗战事冲击，出口大国反进口：卡塔尔与美国签年购400万吨LNG至2054年长单

Source PDF: all report pdf/伊朗战事冲击，出口大国反进口：卡塔尔与美国签年购400万吨LNG至2054年长单.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

卡塔尔达成⾸份对美 LNG 承购协议，美国最⼤ LNG 出⼝商切尼尔能源将从 2031 年开始交
付。因国内⼯业城在冲突期间受损，加之霍尔⽊兹海峡航运动荡，卡塔尔要维持客⼾合同
承诺不得不⽤美国产能补⾃家缺⼝。
摘要：
作为伊朗战事前全球名列前茅的液化天然⽓（ LNG ）出⼝国，卡塔尔⾸次以买⽅⾝份向美国采
购天然⽓，全球能源贸易流向被地缘冲突撕开了⼀道罕⻅的裂⼝。
美东时间 22 ⽇周四，卡塔尔国有油⽓公司卡塔尔能源（ QatarEnergy ）宣布，与美国最⼤ LNG 出
⼝商切尼尔能源（ Cheniere Energy ）达成了⼆⼗余年内每年最⾼采购 400 万吨 LNG 的⻓期协
议。据报道，这是卡塔尔⾸份对美 LNG 承购协议。
促成这笔交易的，是卡塔尔⾃⾝供应体系的断裂。国内 Ras Laffan ⼯业城在美以伊冲突期间受
损，卡塔尔能源被迫⼤幅减少出⼝流量，叠加霍尔⽊兹海峡航运持续动荡，卡塔尔需向亚洲客
⼾维持合同承诺，不得不借助美国的产能填补⾃家的缺⼝。
对切尼尔⽽⾔，⼀份来⾃主权级买家的超⻓期承购协议，为美国 LNG 的定价权与市场地位提供

了新的背书。对全球市场⽽⾔，中东出⼝国开始反向进⼝，供应链脆弱性正从⻛险概念变为现
实。
⾸份对美⻓单：两座液化⼚各 200 万吨
卡塔尔能源称，与切尼尔能源公司（ Cheniere ）签署了两份⻓期购销协议（ SPA ），采购量最⾼
可达每年 400 万吨液化天然⽓（ LNG ）。
卡塔尔能源将从切尼尔位于美国路易斯安那州 Sabine Pass 与得州 Corpus Christi 的两座液化⼚
每年各采购 200 万吨，其中， Sabine Pass ⼯⼚每年 100 万吨的供应量取决于该设施扩建阶段的
最终投资决定，协议整体框架已经确⽴。
根据协议条款，交付⽅式为离岸价（ FOB ）模式，预计交付将于 2031 年开始，持续⾄ 2054 年。
卡塔尔能源同时也是美国得州 Golden Pass LNG 出⼝项⽬的⼤股东，该项⽬正处于启动阶段，
埃克森美孚（ ExxonMobil ）为另⼀股东。这意味着卡塔尔在美国 LNG 产业链中的⻆⾊，正从资
产持有者扩展为实际承购⽅。
伊朗战事导致卡塔尔供给断裂

卡塔尔的困境源于两个叠加因素。其⼀是卡塔尔东北海岸的 Ras Laffan ⼯业城在伊朗战事中遭
袭， LNG 、氦⽓及尿素等⽣产持续中断；其⼆是霍尔⽊兹海峡通航的安全形势恶化，出⼝量⼤
幅减少。
据⼈⺠⽇报报道，国际海事组织秘书⻓阿塞尼奥·多明格斯⽇前表⽰，霍尔⽊兹海峡及周边海
域安全形势持续恶化，美以伊冲突以来已有超 110 艘船舶在海峡及周边遭袭，⾄少 24 名海员遇
难。 10 ⽉ 5 ⽇⾄ 11 ⽇，海峡及周边报告 12 起船只遇袭事件，创 2 ⽉底以来单周最⾼纪录，仅 11 ⽇
单⽇就有 6 艘船舶遇袭。有统计显⽰，海峡航运量已降⾄冲突前的不⾜⼀成。
卡塔尔能源已将 LNG 交付的不可抗⼒条款延⻓⾄ 11 ⽉，显⽰交付中断仍在持续。

### Page 3

原油
(株)日本総合研究所 原油市場展望 2026年９月
◆現状：100ドルを挟んで乱高下
８月のWTI原油先物価格は 、中旬に80ドル台
後半に上昇。米国とイランの交渉が難航したほか 、
イエメンの武装組織フーシがタンカー等を攻撃したこと
で、石油供給の減少が意識されたことが背景。下旬
には、80ドル台前半に下落 。ウォーシュ議長のタカ
派的な発言を受けて、FRBの利上げ観測が強まり、
米国需要が伸び悩むとの見方が広がったことが主因 。
８月末から９月中旬にかけて 、原油価格は100
ドル台に急騰。米国とイランの間で攻撃の応酬が続
いたことが背景。加えて、フーシがバブ・エル・マンデブ
海峡付近の港湾都市を掌握したことや 、サウジアラ
ビアの東西パイプラインが攻撃を受けて操業を停止し
たことで、ホルムズ海峡を迂回するルートからの石油
輸出が停滞するとの懸念も価格を押し上げ。下旬に
は、東西パイプラインの輸送能力が早期に回復する
との報道を材料に、原油価格は90ドル台に下落。
◆投機筋のネットポジションは小幅に拡大

投機筋の買い越し幅は拡大。中東情勢の緊迫化
を受けて、原油価格の上昇を見込む投資家が増加
している可能性。
◆見通し：当面100ドル前後で高止まり
両国の戦闘がこれ以上激化しないことを前提とす
るメインシナリオでは、原油価格が100ドルを中心と
する高値圏で推移する 見通し。ホルムズ海峡の封
鎖は続く一方、東西パイプラインなどの代替ルートや 、
中東産油国の生産が徐々に回復することが背景。

### Page 4

原油
(株)日本総合研究所 原油市場展望 2026年９月3
◆米国のガソリン・ディーゼル価格が高騰
米国では 、燃料油の価格が高騰 。足元のレギュ
ラーガソリン小売価格は全米平均で 4.5ドル/ガロン
と、消費者の心理的節目とされる４ドルを超える水
準。トラックや農業機械の燃料となるディーゼルの価
格は、統計開始以来の最高値に上昇。
◆製油業者のマージン確保や増税も影響
こうした石油製品価格が高騰する一因は 、原料と
なる原油の価格上昇。ただし、次の２点が、過去の
原油高局面よりも製品価格を高める方向に作用。
第１に、製油業者による強気のマージン設定 。世
界的な石油製品の需給ひっ迫が主因 。ホルムズ海
峡の封鎖を受けて 、中東からの製品輸出が減少し
たほか、主に中東産原油を精製していたアジアからの
輸出も減少。ウクライナによる石油関連インフラへの
攻撃を背景に、ロシアからの輸出も下振れ。
第２に、州政府による増税。連邦政府は、1993
年の増税以降、燃料税を据え置いている一方、多く
の州は、環境対策などの観点から増税を実施 。直

近の原油高局面（2022年）に比べて、州政府に
よる増税分が小売価格を押し上げる構図に。
◆燃料高は、米中間選挙で共和党の逆風に
燃料価格の高騰は、11月に実施される米中間選
挙において、現在の与党である共和党の逆風となる
可能性。ガソリン高が無党派層の不満を強めるほか、
ディーゼル高がトラック運転手や農家などの共和党の
伝統的な支持基盤の反発を招く可能性も。
トピック：米国のガソリン・ディーゼル価格が高騰する背景

晶圓代工 
台積電 (2330 TT)

維持評等  
買進(Buy) 
更 充 裕 的 成 長 信 心 來 自 於 更 明 朗 的 建 廠 展 望 
⚫ 3Q26 、4Q26 毛利 率受 N2 量產 影響 可能持續季減  
⚫ 短期展望無 太大變化，然中長期 產 能保持積極擴充步調  
⚫ 建廠能見度 持續延伸 提供 產業 上行 週期 足夠韌性  
3Q26 獲利表現仍佳 ：台積電 3Q26 營收達 1.49 兆元(QoQ+ 17.6 %、
YoY+ 50.9 %)，超越財測上緣 ；毛利率 受制 N2 稀釋影響為 66.4% 、營
益率 58.7% 、EPS 29.4 元，除毛利率外均優於市場預期 。 
4Q26 營收 續強 ，惟 獲利 率指引 呈現 季減 ：公司預期 4Q26 美元 營收 介
於 523~543 億美元 (中值 QoQ+1 4%)，以匯率 31.8 計算台幣營收 落於
16,631 ~17,267 億元 ，優於 市場共識。 然毛利率持續受 N2 放量 、及海
外廠擴張影響， 毛利率 65~67% 、營益率 56.5~58 .5%，中值 均 呈 季減。 
維持 全年 Capex 展望但上修 美元營收 年增 率：隨 4Q26 展望底定，公
司上修 2026 年美元營收年增率 達 44%，Capex 則維持 600~640 億美元
區間不變，但仍預期 2026~2028 年 Capex 投入將遠高於過去三年。此
外，因應緊迫的 N3 需求，除台南新廠規劃 1H27 量產，現再 於當地 增
加一座新廠， 並預期 1H28 量產 ；至於 AZ P2 已進入進機階段，量產
時程也將提前至 3Q27 ；熊本 P2 則規劃 於 2028 年量產 。 
建 廠 能 見 度 持 續 延 伸 ， 有 利 台 積 電 營 運 動 能 更 具 韌 性： 我們訪查顯
示，當前台積電 明確 建廠展望已至少達 2030 年，甚至台灣 /美國潛在
forecast 已分別延伸至 2036/2032 年之久 。而德州雖基於 人力考量，目
前認為難在 2032 前展開，但仍可視為我們對建廠延續性的有力佐證 。
我們亦根據廠務 端建置時程 ， 預估公司 N2/N3/CoWoS 2025~2028 年擴
產 CAGR 分別達 77%/20%/43% ；A14/SoIC/CoPoS 等技術也可望於 2028
年起加速貢獻， 有利支持台積電 2026~2028 年營收 CAGR 逾 33% 。 
雖本次法說 驚喜有限但中長期展望積極 ，維持 「買進」 建議 ：我們認
為雖本次法說相對驚喜有限 、及短期獲利率表現較為平淡， 但在更多
建廠展望確立下，已凸顯本輪 半導體上行 週期具足夠韌性 ，而台積電
領先地位將使其享受多數紅利 。因此我們 維持 對其 「買進」 評等 ，目
標價 隨 EPS 調升上修至 3,330 元(2027 年 EPS*2 2 倍 PER) 。 
風險因子： AI 投資 放緩 。

建廠 可確立 能見度已至少延伸至 2030 年： 根據 我們 訪查 ，近一季 整體台積
電建案均如期進行，其中美國建廠時間已逐步縮減，現預期每 場廠務周期 約
1-1.5 年(原 1.5 -2 年)。此外，我們本次也提供更多廠區 (如 Fab26) 可能 時程預
估(參考圖表二 )，並認為當前台積電較為確立的建廠展望已至少達 2030 年，
甚至台灣 /美國潛在 forecast 已分別延伸至 2036/203 2 年之久 ，顯示本輪 AI
周期延續性具 足夠 韌性 。關於 德州建廠， 雖公司 已正式提及，但 我們 預期最
快也是 2032 年後之規劃 ，主因台積電才剛購入 AZ 第二塊土地 、 及新增1,000
億美元投資， 故推估 AZ 當地至少總計有 9 座 Fab+3 座 AP 計畫 (或最後 也有
機會是 10~11 座 Fab+2 座 AP)，加上人力緊缺問題亦持續升溫， 故實際發生
時間仍需時日。

擴產速度仍有機會上修 ：我們 根據自身廠務追蹤進度，更新台積電先進製程
/先進封裝擴產展望如下， 因 2028 年時間較遠，我們 以當前 建廠 時程進度 、
機台交期狀況 進行產能推估， 當然，後續如進展順利便還有機會上修 。N2/N3
方面，預估 2028 年分別達 195k/22 5k、對應 2025~2028 年擴產 CAGR 分別
達 77%/20%；CoWoS 2028 年預估產能 220k，對應 2025~2028 年擴產 CAGR
為 43%，我們也觀察到短期因良率 未到滿足點 、 客戶需求問題， 故 AP7 P3/P4
將優先 調整為生產 CoWoS( 但仍有配置 for SoIC 的特氣管路 )， 也因此小幅下
修 SoIC 2027 年擴產展望至 30k、2028 年實際展望也尚待商榷 (初步預估 40k)。

投資建議 
展望明後年，光纖陣列與稜鏡微透鏡陣列等
光學元件新業務增長動能明確，將彌補手機
鏡頭缺緩造成的缺口，帶動大立光營運獲利
重返成長軌道。合庫預估 2026 / 2027 年
獲利分別衰退 1% / 成長 7% ，稅後每股盈
餘 158.03 / 169.79  元，建議操作區間  
5,435 ~ 7,300 元 (2027 PER 32 ~ 43x)。 
業務營收比重

公司簡介  
大立光電股份有限公司創立於 1987 年 ， 專業
於各式光學鏡頭模組與光電零組件等產品之
研究開發，產品包括智慧型手機鏡頭、3D 結
構光鏡頭、ToF、屏下光學指紋、空拍機鏡頭、
平板電腦鏡頭、IP 相機鏡頭、智慧型電視鏡
頭、AR/VR 鏡頭 、IoT 鏡頭 、 穿戴式裝置鏡頭 、
虹膜辨識鏡頭、醫療儀器用鏡頭、汽車鏡頭、
鏡片等光電零組件之設計、製造與加工。

2

營運展望 
1. 3Q26 良率與成本明顯衝擊毛利率表現：受到綠電成本上升以及新機良率較低 ，
造成毛利率低於市場預期。第三季營收 157 億元、+14.5% QoQ、-11.5% YoY，
毛利率 42.2%。 營業利益 47 億元、-8.0% QoQ、-24.4% YoY，營業利益率 30.3%。
歸屬母公司稅後淨利 52 億元，每股盈餘 39.62 元。產品組合分別為 2,000 
萬畫素以上鏡頭佔比 10 ~ 20%、1,000 萬畫素鏡頭佔比 60 ~ 70%、800 萬畫
素鏡頭佔比小於 10%、低於 500 萬畫素鏡頭佔比 20 ~ 30%。 
2. 4Q26 春季新品拉貨動能仍無法彌補旗艦機種下修的缺口 ：第四季拉貨動能趨
緩，公司預期 10 月拉貨動能與 9 月相當，但 11 月拉貨動能開始下滑。即
使春季中階新品開始備貨，仍無法彌補旗艦機種下修的缺口。不過可變光圈良
率已經明顯改善 ， 有助毛利率拉升 。 但綠電成本部分 ， 已成為結構性負面因素 ，
最快需至後年才能漲價彌補差異。 
3. 光纖陣列已通過客戶驗證，預期 2027 年下半年產能可建置完成上線：公司證
實光纖陣列 (Fiber Array, FA) 產品已於九月順利取得終端客戶認證。十月
將與客戶正式洽談具體產能規模需求，預估產線最快 2027 年年中能建置完成
並開始量產。至於光纖陣列 (Fiber Array Unit, FAU）模組現階段尚未進入送
樣流程，而是透過產線運作與現場生產數據供客戶評估，本月將與客戶協商確
切送樣與認證時程。 目前聚焦自製  FA 並外購 稜鏡微透鏡陣列  (Photonic 
Module Lens Assembly, PMLA ）進行耦合以組裝成 FAU 模組。下一階段目標
為爭取載板 (carrier) 組裝業務 ， 以深化產品附加價值 。 至於近期購入的七座
廠房土地將全數投入建置生產線及無塵室，預計明年開工，2030 年完工。客戶
若採用 NPO 方案 ， 只要非採用 VCSEL 技術 ， 其所需元件與公司現行的 FAU 解
決方案幾乎一致，技術差異僅存在於封裝形式，公司皆能通用並持續供貨。 
4. 手機市場雖趨緩，但光纖業務展望佳將助營運重返成長：展望明後年，光纖陣
列與稜鏡微透鏡陣列等光學元件新業務增長動能明確，將彌補受科技通膨影響
的手機鏡頭業務缺口，帶動大立光營運獲利重返成長軌道。合庫預估 2026 年
營收 598 億元 、-2.2% YoY， 毛利率 46.8%。 營業利益 208 億元 、-11.6% YoY，
營業利益率 34.8%。歸屬母公司稅後淨利 207 億元，每股盈餘 158.03 元。 
5. ESG (大立光在 2026 年上市公司治理評鑑結果為 66 ~ 80%)：大立光 ESG 委
員會為跨部門溝通平台，負責規畫與執行及管控各項行動方案，整合與監督公
司治理 (含經濟)、社會、環境三大面向的執行進度與成效，確保組織橫向與縱
向溝通的有效性，具體實踐永續發展。大立光依據六大篩選原則「責任」 、 「影
響力」 、 「親近度」 、 「依賴性」 、 「代表性」 、 「政策及策略意圖」 ，在 ESG 委員會
會議鑑別出五個主要的利害關係人 ， 包含投資人 (股東)、 員工 、 客戶 、 供應商 、
政府機關。

市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂

积极增强⼈⼯智能创造就业机会、赋能传统岗位、提升就业质量、激发创业活⼒的正⾯效
应，有效减缓⼈⼯智能应⽤就业替代影响，努⼒实现技术进步、产业升级与就业促进、⺠
⽣改善相协调的包容性发展。
摘要：
国务院办公厅⽇前印发《关于适应⼈⼯智能技术发展助⼒构建就业友好型发展⽅式的意⻅》（以
下简称《意⻅》），对相关⼯作作出部署。
《意⻅》强调，要坚持以习近平新时代中国特⾊社会主义思想为指导，深⼊贯彻党的⼆⼗⼤和
⼆⼗届历次全会精神，坚持以⼈⺠为中⼼的发展思想，坚持投资于物和投资于⼈紧密结合，坚
持统筹发展和安全，坚持就业优先导向，以⼈⼯智能赋能⾼质量发展为契机，以适应技术发展
促进⾼质量充分就业为⽬标，以构建就业友好型发展⽅式为⽅向，积极增强⼈⼯智能创造就业
机会、赋能传统岗位、提升就业质量、激发创业活⼒的正⾯效应，有效减缓⼈⼯智能应⽤就业

替代影响，努⼒实现技术进步、产业升级与就业促进、⺠⽣改善相协调的包容性发展。
《意⻅》部署了 6 个⽅⾯ 18 项主要任务。⼀是强化⼈⼯智能的就业创造效应。拓展新兴领域⼈⼯
智能就业新空间，培育中⼩微企业稳岗扩岗新动能，创造⼈⼯智能领域就业创业新模式，加快
开发⼈⼯智能领域新职业。⼆是构建就业友好型⼈⼯智能升级路径。挖掘传统领域⼈⼯智能就
业潜能，减缓⼈⼯智能应⽤就业替代影响，建⽴⼈⼯智能应⽤就业⻛险评估机制。三是提升劳
动者数字素养和就业能⼒。协同培养⼈⼯智能创新型⼈才，实施⼈⼯智能专项培训计划。四是
推动⼈⼯智能赋能⾼品质就业政策服务。运⽤⼈⼯智能助⼒就业政策落实，提⾼就业服务智能
化⽔平，加⼤转型转岗就业⽀持⼒度。五是健全劳动者权益保障制度和社会保障制度。优化适
应⼈机协同的劳动者权益保障制度，完善灵活就业、新就业形态劳动者社会保障制度，健全适
应⼈⼯智能技术发展的收⼊分配机制。六是完善⼈⼯智能应⽤就业监测预警和⻛险应对体系。
强化⼈⼯智能应⽤就业⻛险监测预警，加强⼈⼯智能应⽤就业影响研究，健全⼈⼯智能应⽤就

业⻛险应对机制。
《意⻅》要求，坚持把党的领导贯彻到适应⼈⼯智能技术发展促进就业创业⼯作全过程各⽅
⾯。各地区各有关部⻔要结合实际抓好本意⻅贯彻落实，进⼀步强化就业优先政策导向，统筹
考虑⼈⼯智能应⽤就业影响，加快构建就业友好型发展⽅式。各级⼈⼒资源社会保障、发展改
⾰部⻔要加强统筹协调，推动各项政策措施落地⻅效，加强宣传引导，营造有利于促进就业创
业的良好社会氛围。

### Page 1

宏观经济周报 - 2026 年 10 月 20 日 1 
证券研究 
宏观经济周报 
2026 年第四十三周

一、中国宏观 
高频：本周综合景气指数录得 99.35，较上周明显回落，并重新降至荣枯线下
方，显示经济景气在前期企稳后出现阶段性调整。考虑到本周处于国庆假期期
间，部分生产、投资及项目施工活动受到假期安排影响，景气指数的回落具有
一定季节性特征。 从分项结构来看， 消费景气继续保持扩张，而投资、出口和
生产景气均处于荣枯线下方，其中生产景气回落最为明显，是拖累本周综合指
数下行的主要因素。 消费方面，景气指数录得 100.19，较上周小幅上升，并继
续运行于荣枯线之上，显示居民消费需求总体保持韧性。国庆假期期间出行、
餐饮及文旅等消费需求释放，对消费景气形成一定支撑，消费端仍是当前经济
景气中表现相对稳定的领域。 投资方面， 景气指数录得 99.77，较上周有所回
升，但仍低于荣枯线，表明投资活动边际有所改善，但整体动能仍然偏弱。考
虑到假期期间部分项目施工和资金投放节奏有所放缓，本周投资景气表现也可
能受到一定季节性扰动。 外需方面，出口景气指数录得 99.95，较上周小幅回
升，继续运行于荣枯线附近，显示外部需求总体保持稳定。出口景气近期波动
幅度有限，表明外需尚未出现明显走弱，但增长动能仍相对有限。 生产方面，
景气指数降至 98.28，较上周明显回落，并大幅低于荣枯线，是本周综合景气走
弱的主要拖累。由于本周处于国庆假期，企业开工、物流运输及订单执行节奏
阶段性放缓，可能对生产景气形成较明显扰动，因此本周生产端回落更多需要
结合假期因素观察。后续随着企业复工复产恢复，生产景气能否快速回升将成
为判断本轮调整持续性的关键。总体来看，本周综合景气有所走弱，但国庆假
期带来的季节性扰动是重要影响因素，尤其体现在生产和投资端。与此同时，
消费仍保持扩张，出口亦基本稳定在临界水平附近，需求端整体尚未出现明显
转弱。随着节后生产经营活动逐步恢复，综合景气有望重新向荣枯线附近修
复。 
2026 年 9 月，中国 CPI 同比上涨 0.9%，环比上涨 0.2%，核心 CPI 同比上涨
1.0%，终端价格保持温和上涨。 CPI 同比回升主要由能源价格推动，其中能源
价格涨幅由 4.1%扩大至 7.3%，汽油价格上涨 16.6%，拉动 CPI 约 0.47 个百分
点。食品价格下降 1.7%，服务价格上涨 0.8%，剔除能源的工业消费品价格涨幅
有所回落。 9 月 PPI 同比上涨 5.0%，较上月扩大 1.2 个百分点。环比上涨
1.2%，涨幅扩大 0.8 个百分点。国际原油、有色金属价格上行是主要推动因素，
煤炭备货需求、建筑材料需求改善以及人工智能相关产业发展也形成支撑。煤
炭、石油化工、有色金属、电气机械和电子设备等八个行业合计拉动 PPI 同比
上涨 5.42 个百分点，表明价格上涨仍较集中于上游资源品和部分新兴产业。

图 1：ICHI 指数反映当下经济运行的冷热程度 
资料来源：Wind、iFinD 以及工银国际计算

二、全球宏观 
10 月 14 日，美国公布 9 月 CPI，整体 CPI 环比上涨 0.6%，同比上涨 3.7%，较 8
月进一步加快；核心 CPI 环比上涨 0.3%，同比上涨 2.6%。能源是本月通胀上升
的主要来源，其中汽油价格单月上涨 7.1%。但除能源之外，住房、航空票价以
及部分消费商品价格同样有所上涨，说明价格压力并非完全由油价单独推动。
与年中相比，美国核心通胀仍明显低于整体通胀，但整体价格水平重新加速，
使此前较为顺畅的去通胀进程再次出现波动。 
10 月 15 日，美国公布 9 月 PPI，最终需求 PPI 环比大幅上涨 1.3%，同比升至
6.3%，为四年多以来最明显的一次月度上涨。商品价格是主要推动因素，其中
汽油价格环比上涨 20.3%，柴油价格上涨约 21%；与此同时，运输、法律服务
和住宿等服务价格也出现上升。相比 8 月，本月生产端通胀的广度明显扩大，
不仅反映能源成本冲击，也说明物流和服务环节价格仍在上行。PPI 的大幅上涨
意味着美国企业在生产、运输和中间服务环节承受的成本压力继续增加。

三、市场聚焦 
10 月 15 日，美国 9 月零售销售环比增长 0.8%，高于市场预期，但其中较大部
分增幅来自汽油价格上涨。剔除汽车、汽油、建材和餐饮后的核心零售销售仅
增长 0.1%，明显低于 8 月的 1.2%。这表明名义消费金额仍然增长，但实际消费
动能已经有所分化。高收入家庭消费仍具有韧性，而低收入家庭在能源和生活
成本上升背景下更加谨慎。美国居民消费尚未明显收缩，但从零售结构看，消
费增长的广度有所减弱。 
10 月 15 日，美国最新初请失业金人数小幅升至 19.9 万人，但已经连续第四周

### Page 3

宏观经济周报 - 2026 年 10 月 20 日 3
证券研究
低于 20 万人。企业裁员规模总体仍然较低，续请失业金人数也小幅下降。不
过，美国就业市场仍表现出较典型的 “低招聘、低裁员 ”特征：企业不愿大规模
削减现有员工，但新增招聘意愿同样有限，失业者重新找到工作的时间有所延
长。当前劳动力市场不像典型衰退时期那样出现快速恶化，但就业流动性较此
前明显下降。

Document 648: 市场日报：三大指数午后回落 美国9月PPI通胀大超预期

### Page 1

证券研究报告——市场日报

大同证券研究中心 
分析师：景剑文 
执业证书编号：S0770523090001

周五（10 月 16 日） ， 三大指数早盘高开，
之后分化，沪指震荡拉升，深指、创指短暂回
落后拉升，午盘市场行情回落，三大指数震荡
走低，悉数回落，尾盘触底反弹，沪指翻红，
深指、创指收绿。 截至收盘， 上证指数 （+0.24%）
收涨 3716.28 点，深证成指（-0.12%）收跌
11802.46 点， 创业板指 （-0.39%）收跌 2772.38
点， 两市量能达到 1.5 万亿。 申万一级行业多
数上涨，国防军工、房地产、农林牧渔领涨，
传媒、医药生物、非银金融领跌。

外围突发利空引发市场扰动

美国 9 月PPI 通胀大超预期 能源冲击向更多领域扩散 美联储加息预期再升温。美国劳工部
周四公布的数据显示，美国 9 月生产者价格指数(PPI)涨幅远超市场预期，凸显在美国与伊
朗战争持续之际， 能源价格上涨仍在加剧美国通胀压力。数据显示， 衡量上游通胀压力的PPI
在 9 月环比上涨 1.3%，创 2022 年 3 月以来新高，预期值为 0.5%，前值为 0.4%；同比上涨
6.3%，创2022 年 12 月以来新高，预期值为 5.3%，前值为 5.4%。（消息来源：东方财富） 
一线城市商品住房销售制度改革细则全面落地 多地楼市稳步回暖。10 月 15 日， 深圳市住房
和建设局、深圳市规划和自然资源局、国家金融监督管理总局深圳监管局三部门联合印发关
于贯彻落实《关于完善商品住房销售制度的通知》的实施意见，并自发布之日起施行。（消
息来源：东方财富） 
IEA 成员国将尽快释放逾 2000 万桶柴油 以应对全球燃料价格飙升。国际能源署(IEA)周四
在一份声明中表示，其成员国已同意“尽快”释放逾 2000 万桶柴油，并加快释放今年 3 月
承诺动用的创纪录紧急石油储备中尚未投放市场的部分， 以应对全球燃料价格大幅上涨的局
面。（消息来源：东方财富）

Document 688: 油价“更高更久”成新常态？美伊再度剑拔弩张，明年油价回落希望迅速破灭

Source PDF: all report pdf/油价“更高更久”成新常态？美伊再度剑拔弩张，明年油价回落希望迅速破灭.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

中东航运⻛险持续发酵，原油出⼝恢复仍难消除供应隐忧， 2027 年原油期货全线⾛⾼。更
危险的是，⽯油库存缓冲不断消耗，⼀旦⾼油价延续⾄明年，能源冲击或从短期扰动演变
为持久通胀压⼒，令全球经济与央⾏政策再度承压。市场叙事正从 “ 暂时性 ” 转向 “ 更⾼更久 ”
的新常态。
摘要：
全球原油市场对明年油价回落的期待正在迅速降温。随着中东供应中断持续、航运安全⻛险居
⾼不下，交易员开始押注油价将在更⻓时间内维持⾼位。即使波斯湾原油出⼝量已经恢复⾄战
前⽔平以上，运输环节的瓶颈与额外成本仍在制约供应恢复，市场此前期待的能源价格回落，
正⾯临越来越⼤的不确定性。
2027 年原油期货价格已升⾄伊朗战争爆发以来的最⾼⽔平。⾃ 9 ⽉初以来，2027 年 1 ⽉交割的原
油期货上涨 17% ，突破 97 美元 / 桶； 6 ⽉合约上涨 18% ⾄ 88 美元， 12 ⽉合约上涨 13% ⾄约 81 美
元。尽管市场仍然预期明年油价会有所缓解，但与⼏周前相⽐，这种乐观预期已明显减弱。
这⼀变化与夏末的市场情绪形成鲜明对⽐。当时，随着市场期待伊朗局势缓和、中东原油供应

恢复，远期油价⼀度回落。如今，持续的地缘政治⻛险正在改变交易员对供应恢复时间的判
断。
Unlimited Funds ⾸席投资官 Bob Elliot 周三表⽰，市场叙事正在眼前从 “ 暂时性 ” 转向 “ 新常
态 ” 。此外，德意志银⾏能源分析师  Michael Hsueh 认为，如果战争持续⾄ 2027 年，油价则可
能向 130 美元 / 桶迈进。
更值得关注的是，油价⾼企的影响可能不⽌于能源市场。战争爆发以来，原本能够缓冲中东供
应中断的⽯油库存已被⼤量消耗。如果⾼油价持续⾄明年，能源冲击就可能从⼀次性的价格变
化演变为更持久的通胀压⼒，令全球经济增⻓和央⾏利率决策⾯临更⼤挑战。
原油出⼝恢复，运输瓶颈仍未解除
从原油出⼝数据看，中东供应并未出现与市场担忧完全对应的持续性萎缩。据《华尔街⽇报》
报道，截⾄ 10 ⽉ 18 ⽇当周，包括经霍尔⽊兹海峡及替代航线运输的原油在内，波斯湾出⼝量已
经超过战前⽔平。
但出⼝量恢复，并不意味着供应链已回归正常。为维持原油运输，市场不得不采⽤成本更⾼、

操作更复杂的替代⽅案，其中包括在霍尔⽊兹海峡外进⾏船对船转运。与此同时，安全⻛险已
经令部分船只望⽽却步。
Energy Aspects 全球⽯油业务负责⼈ Christopher Haines 向 Axios 表⽰，即便采取这些替代措
施，中东原油的实际外运量也正在触及上限。他还指出，要重新建⽴航运公司和保险商对船只
不会遭受袭击的信⼼，将 “ 极为困难 ” 。
这揭⽰了当前油市⾯临的核⼼⽭盾：原油仍然可以⽣产和出⼝，但将其安全、稳定地运抵市
场，变得更加困难。只要航运安全⽆法得到保障，替代运输⽅案就难以⽆限扩张，出⼝量也可
能受到制约。
因此，单看原油出⼝数据，容易低估中东局势对全球能源市场的影响。即便实际供应有所恢
复，运输能⼒和安全信⼼的缺⼝仍可能使市场保持紧张。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

油价反映的不只是供应短缺，还有恐慌溢价
当前油价上涨，并不完全意味着全球市场缺少同等规模的原油。经济学家 Robin Brooks 认为，
运输成本上升以及市场对进⼀步袭击的担忧，⽐实际原油短缺更能解释当前的价格表现。
根据此前的⽉度数据， Brooks 估算，波斯湾原油出⼝已恢复⾄战前⽔平的 91% ，但市场中的 “ 暂
时性恐慌因素 ” ⾄少为油价增加了 20 美元 / 桶。
这意味着，即使原油出⼝逐步恢复，市场也未必会⽴即降低对供应⻛险的定价。航运企业能否
恢复正常运营、保险商是否愿意承保，以及船只遇袭⻛险是否继续上升，都会影响原油运输的
成本和效率。
⽽这类⻛险难以通过增加产量迅速解决。只要市场仍担忧中东局势可能进⼀步恶化，原油价格
就可能继续包含额外的⻛险溢价。
与此同时，远期合约持续上涨，说明交易员正在将这种不确定性纳⼊明年的价格预期。市场并
⾮完全放弃油价回落的可能，⽽是不再相信价格能够像此前预期的那样迅速恢复正常。
库存缓冲消退，⾼油价可能拖累通胀与增⻓
原油市场的另⼀重压⼒来⾃库存缓冲的消耗。

战争爆发以来，⽯油库存曾帮助市场应对中东供应中断，减轻实际供应减少对价格的冲击。但
随着这⼀缓冲⼤幅消耗，如果供应⻛险持续，市场抵御下⼀轮能源冲击的能⼒也将下降。
这将给央⾏带来更棘⼿的政策问题。传统上，央⾏通常会将能源价格上涨视为⼀次性的相对价
格变化，并在判断中暂时淡化其影响，前提是能源冲击不会演变为持续性的通胀过程。
但如果油价在明年仍维持⾼位，这⼀判断逻辑就可能受到挑战。能源成本持续上涨，将使经济
更容易受到新⼀轮价格冲击影响，其对通胀、经济增⻓和利率的影响也可能⽐以往更为持久。
换⾔之，油价 “ 更⾼更久 ” 不仅意味着消费者和企业需要继续承受⾼能源成本，也意味着央⾏可能
更难判断能源冲击究竟何时才会消退。
美国试图压低燃油价格，额外供应空间却有限
⾯对燃油价格压⼒，特朗普政府正寻求增加市场供应，以缓解中期选举前的能源成本压⼒。最
新举措包括允许更多俄罗斯柴油进⼊市场。
但这⼀政策能否带来明显的价格缓解，仍存在疑问。
Haines 表⽰，乌克兰对俄罗斯炼油⼚的袭击已经造成损坏，⽽制裁⼜增加了相关设施修复的难

度，限制了俄罗斯进⼀步增加柴油出⼝的空间。因此，额外供应对市场的缓解作⽤可能⼗分有
限。
这也意味着，单纯寻找替代供应来源，未必⾜以迅速扭转市场对能源价格的预期。如果中东运
输⻛险持续，⽽其他来源的额外供应⼜受到限制，原油市场就难以获得⾜够的缓冲。
眼下，交易员仍然预期 2027 年油价会有所回落，但远期合约持续⾛⾼、库存缓冲不断消耗，以
及运输⻛险迟迟难以消退，都在削弱这⼀预期。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

研究所

分析师:李起 
SAC 登记编号:S1340524110001

分析师:高晓洁

近期研究报告

《海外宏观周报：加息预期降温，十月
或按兵不动》 - 2026.10.12   
 宏观观点

海外宏观周报：中选或加剧分化，关注非科技机会

⚫ 核心观点 
美股企稳需要看到长端利率进一步回落，以及 AI 相关企业的信
用压力不再恶化。 近期美债收益率虽有所下降， 但融资成本仍然偏高，
若 AI 企业信用利差继续走阔，市场对资本开支持续性和盈利兑现的
担忧也难以消退。我们认为，短期美股仍将维持震荡，当前不宜急于
抄底。中东局势及美国中期选举的不确定性仍然较高，需要等待金融
条件和企业盈利预期出现更明确的改善。结构上，此前资金和持仓高
度集中于AI相关股票，非科技板块可能先于科技股企稳。 
中期选举方面，尽管市场对民主党同时控制参众两院的预期升
温，但历史经验显示市场往往在中期选举前高估在野党横扫概率。我
们仍以民主党取得众议院、共和党保留参议院控制权作为基准情形。
中期选举对财政力度的影响并不明确，但OBBBA的收缩效应将逐年显
现。共和党或在中期选举后通过协调机制扩大预算。产业政策方面，
AI数据中心建设存在较大的放缓风险。 决定数据中心政策环境的权力
已经被下放到地方，而当前民主党在数据中心核心州的州长和议员竞
选上占有优势，尽管联邦层面监管措施出台的概率较小。医疗领域则
可能迎来政策改善，民主党或利用明年的债务上限谈判争取延长医疗
补贴、减轻削减力度，改善居民医疗支付能力，利好医院及部分健康
保险公司。

⚫ 风险提示： 
若中东局势缓和，通胀回落带动长端利率较快下降，科技龙头业
绩超预期，美股可能提前企稳，科技股或重新领涨。若选举后医疗补
贴延长受阻，医院及健康保险公司的政策受益预期可能落空。

发布时间：2026-10-20

1 海外宏观事件与数据跟踪 
上周中东能源运输风险持续上升。霍尔木兹海峡附近商船遇袭事件增多，船
对船转运等替代运输方式也受到威胁。周末，伊朗革命卫队称，两艘油轮在穿越
海峡时触雷起火。与此同时，伊朗表示已通过卡塔尔提出有条件重新开放海峡的
方案，航运安全与外交谈判仍存在较大不确定性。为缓解供应压力，国际能源署
于10月15日宣布，加快落实此前联合释储安排中剩余约1 亿桶库存的释放，其
中包括尽快释放超过2000万桶柴油。 
美国通胀回升，价格压力向更多分项扩散。9月CPI环比上涨0.6%，与市场
预期一致，同比增速由 3.4%升至 3.7%，高于预期的 3.6%；核心 CPI 环比上涨
0.3%，高于市场普遍预期的0.2%，同比增速由2.4%升至 2.6%。能源仍是整体通
胀的重要推手，汽油价格环比上涨9.1%，但核心分项同样表现偏强：住房价格环
比上涨0.4%，二手车、服装、教育及机票价格均有上涨。 
美股 AI 产业链调整加深，信用市场压力向股票市场传导。上周标普500 指
数下跌1.9%，纳斯达克100指数下跌5.5%，罗素2000指数下跌3.0%；半导体、
存储以及AI云服务商等前期强势板块明显承压。市场对AI基础设施融资能力及
盈利兑现的担忧升温，部分企业信用利差走阔，进一步拖累相关股票。资金流向
也趋于防御，医疗保健、必需消费等板块相对占优。债券方面，通胀数据公布后
收益率先升后降，10年期美债收益率周末收于约5.07%， 全周下降约17个基点。

2 美联储观点跟踪 
1.美联储主席沃什： 
10月 16日，沃什在曼谷IMF年会上表示，自9 月议息会议以来，经济走势
总体符合美联储预期。美国正经历显著的投资扩张，这是支撑经济增长的重要因
素，但全球物价上涨也使央行持续面临挑战。他重申兑现价格稳定的承诺，并将
9月加息描述为撤回一部分宽松；对于后续政策，他没有预设10月或12月会议
的决定，也未给出下一次加息的明确时间。 
谈及人工智能，沃什认为，AI投资首先推升总需求，生产率提高和供给能力
扩张则需要更长时间才能体现。他看好 AI 在中期形成积极的供给冲击，同时强
调技术变革伴随尾部风险，需要评估不同情景对物价、就业和金融稳定的影响。

2.克利夫兰联储主席哈玛克： 
哈玛克表示，美国通胀压力较为广泛，能源、关税和AI 建设需求均在推高
成本，部分价格压力已扩展至航空、电力、建筑及核心服务领域。她认为，能源
价格回落有助于缓解通胀，但需求端压力可能更加持久，需要更具限制性的货币
政策应对。 持续高通胀也可能改变企业定价习惯， 使其提前为未来成本上涨提价，
增加通胀预期固化的风险。 
哈玛克认为， 劳动力市场总体稳定， 裁员较少， 部分行业还面临劳动力短缺，
为进一步治理通胀提供了空间。尽管债券收益率已经上升，她仍将整体金融条件
判断为中性， 尚未明显抑制需求。 在政策操作上， 她倾向于及时地进行小幅调整，
以降低后续大幅收紧的风险；若就业出现可信的恶化迹象，政策路径也需要相应
改变。

3.美联储理事沃勒： 
沃勒在新德里参加公开讨论时表示，长期美债收益率上升正在抑制住房、汽
车等利率敏感行业的需求，承担了部分货币政策收紧的作用。即使联邦基金利率
暂时不变，长期融资成本上升也会影响居民和企业的支出决策。他指出，虽然推
动长期收益率上涨并非美联储的直接意图，但这一变化确实有助于降低需求。

### Page 8

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分      8 
4.美联储副主席鲍曼： 
鲍曼在曼谷国际金融协会年会上表示， 通胀仍高于目标， 但在此前加息之后，
她没有感受到立即采取进一步行动的紧迫性。 美联储仍有时间观察经济和金融条
件的变化，再决定是否继续调整政策。 
鲍曼同时关注 AI 扩张带来的融资及金融安全问题。她指出，数据中心建设
正在形成大量资本需求，监管部门需要了解这些项目如何融资，以及资金是否充
足；前沿 AI 模型也可能带来针对关键基础设施的网络安全风险。在银行监管方
面，她支持为金融创新保留空间，并研究将AI用于银行监督和欺诈识别。 
图表9：市场预期今年 12 月还有一次加息

资料来源：Fed Watch，中邮证券研究所

3 风险提示 
若中东局势缓和，通胀回落带动长端利率较快下降，科技龙头业绩超预期，
美股可能提前企稳，科技股或重新领涨。若选举后医疗补贴延长受阻，医院及健
康保险公司的政策受益预期可能落空。

可转债市场以中信标普可转债
指数为基准；香港市场以恒生
指数为基准；美国市场以标普
500 或纳斯达克综合指数为基
准。
股票评级
买入 预期个股相对同期基准指数涨幅在 20%以上
增持 预期个股相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%与 20%之间
中性 预期个股相对同期基准指数涨幅在-10%与 10%之间
回避 预期个股相对同期基准指数涨幅在-10%以下
行业评级
强于大市 预期行业相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上
中性 预期行业相对同期基准指数涨幅在-10%与 10%之间
弱于大市 预期行业相对同期基准指数涨幅在-10%以下

公司简介 
中邮证券有限责任公司于 2002 年9月经中国证券监督管理委员会批准设立， 公司注册资本61.68 亿元人民币，
是中国邮政集团有限公司绝对控股的证券类金融子公司，公司是中邮创业基金管理股份有限公司的第二大股东。 
公司经营范围包括:证券经纪，证券自营，证券投资咨询，证券资产管理，融资融券，证券投资基金销售，证券
承销与保荐，代理销售金融产品，与证券交易、证券投资活动有关的财务顾问，具备展业的各项资格。截至2025 年
10 月底，公司在全国设有58 家分支机构(含 29 家分公司、29 家营业部)，1 家资产管理分公司和 1 家另类投资子公
司。 
中邮证券紧密依托中国邮政集团有限公司的雄厚实力，通过强化“自营+协同”发展模式，实现快速发展，当前
服务的经纪客户已超过 260 万人。公司始终坚持诚信经营、践行金融为民，为社会大众提供全方位专业化的证券投
融资服务，努力成为员工自豪、股东放心、客户信赖、社会尊重的优秀企业，打造契合中国邮政资源禀赋和市场地
位的特色精品券商。

市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
Calpers 资产管理规模达 6371 亿美元，持仓变化本⾝即具有市场信号意义。基⾦在 2026 年
上半年将直接持有的 "Mag 7" 股票总数削减了 26% ，转⽽分散布局数百家公司，同时提⾼私
募股权敞⼝。
摘要：
美国最⼤公共养⽼⾦正在重新布局。加州公务员退休系统（ Calpers ）⼤幅削减对 " 科技七巨
头 " 的持仓，转⽽加码私募股权，这⼀动向折射出机构投资者对美股科技板块⾼估值与集中度⻛
险的深层忧虑。
据英国《⾦融时报》报道， Calpers 副⾸席投资官 Anton Orlich 在采访中证实了这⼀战略转向。
该基⾦在 2026 年上半年将直接持有的 "Mag 7" 股票总数削减了 26% ，转⽽分散布局数百家公司
以降低权益端集中⻛险。与此同时， Calpers 正提升私募股权敞⼝，理由是私募市场估值相对更

具吸引⼒。
此次调仓发⽣于美股科技板块承压之前。本周多家科技巨头财报公布后，市场对⼈⼯智能⽀出
能否转化为实际回报的疑虑升温，科技股遭到集中抛售。微软、亚⻢逊和 Alphabet 的业绩将于
下周相继出炉，科技板块仍⾯临进⼀步考验。
砍仓幅度显著，集中度⻛险成核⼼顾虑
媒体根据监管⽂件测算， Calpers 在 2026 年上半年将直接持有的 " 科技七巨头 "—— 苹果、微软、
Alphabet 、亚⻢逊、英伟达、 Meta 和特斯拉 —— 股票总数削减 26% 。
Anton Orlich 在采访中明确指出，公开股权市场的估值已处于较⾼⽔平，少数头部公司⾼度集
中，相对回报可能承压。这⼀表态直接点明了 Calpers 此次战略调整的出发点：在持续多年的科
技⽜市之后，集中持仓的⻛险收益⽐已不再划算。
Anton Orlich 表⽰，私营公司的定价处于更具吸引⼒的⽔平，这有助于 Calpers 在公开市场出现
下⾏时获得⼀定缓冲。
这⼀逻辑契合当前部分⼤型机构投资者的配置思路 ——在公开市场估值偏⾼、流动性溢价压缩

的环境下，私募股权相对折价提供了更⼤的安全边际。 Calpers 此番将 " ⼤额持有七只股票 " 转换
为 " 分散布局数百家公司 " ，本质上是以流动性换取⻛险分散和潜在超额回报。
体量庞⼤，战略转向影响不容⼩觑
Calpers 是美国规模最⼤的政府雇员养⽼⾦，截⾄今年 6 ⽉ 30 ⽇，资产管理规模达 6371 亿美元。
该基⾦ 7 ⽉公布数据显⽰，过去 12 个⽉初步净投资回报率为 14.8% ，公开和私募股权投资组合均
表现强劲。
正因体量巨⼤， Calpers 的持仓变化本⾝即具有市场信号意义。此次⾼调宣布从 "Mag 7" 撤退并
转向私募，可能进⼀步加剧市场对科技股头部集中度的审视，也将为其他⼤型机构在资产配置
上的类似讨论提供参照。

Document 799: 野村警告：当前AI信用风险与互联网泡沫、雷曼危机前惊人相似，一切都取决于油价

Source PDF: all report pdf/野村警告：当前AI信用风险与互联网泡沫、雷曼危机前惊人相似，一切都取决于油价.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

野村构建 " ⼗年信⽤周期 " 框架，将当前 AI 科技与法国主权信⽤动荡定性为 " 第⼆轮冲击 "——
历史上此阶段结束后约 1-2 年，必爆发引发衰退的 " 第三轮冲击 " 。核⼼变量是油价：若涨价
源于需求过热，美联储将陷⼊滞胀困境、⽆法降息，危机升级难以避免；若主因是地缘政
治，则政策转圜空间犹存。
摘要：
野村证券发出警告，当前围绕美国科技公司和法国的信⽤动荡，与 2000 年互联⽹泡沫崩溃和
2008 年雷曼危机前⼣的信⽤冲击⾼度相似，历史上每⼀次 “ 第⼆轮冲击 ” 之后约⼀⾄两年，都演
变为引发衰退的 “ 第三轮冲击 ”。这⼀次，油价⾛势将成为决定历史是否重演的关键变量。
野村证券策略师 Naka Matsuzawa 在 10 ⽉ 22 ⽇发布的报告中指出，当前信⽤市场的不安情绪，
令⼈联想到 1990 年代 IT 繁荣期间的亚洲货币危机与 LTCM 危机，以及 2000 年代房地产繁荣期间
的次贷危机与⻉尔斯登危机。这些事件均属于其所定义的⼗年信⽤周期中的 “ 第⼆轮冲击 ” 。历史

表明，若美联储能够及时转向降息，经济可在不经历全⾯信⽤危机的情况下重返扩张轨道；但
若油价因经济过热⽽⾮地缘政治因素上涨，央⾏政策空间将被⼤幅压缩，信⽤危机将难以避
免。
Matsuzawa 警告称，美欧央⾏⽬前正因油价上涨⽽持续加息，这⼀背景与历史上 “ 第三轮冲击 ”
前⼣的滞胀困境如出⼀辙，意味着当前的信⽤隐忧存在从局部⻛险演变为系统性危机的可能。
不过，若油价上涨的驱动⼒主要来⾃地缘政治⽽⾮需求端，美联储仍有政策灵活性，可在冲击
发⽣后暂停加息，从⽽阻断危机升级。
历史镜鉴：两次 “ 第⼆轮冲击 ” 如何演变为系统性危机
Matsuzawa 在报告中构建了⼀套⼗年信⽤周期框架，将历史上的重⼤信⽤事件划分为 “ 第⼆轮冲
击 ” 与 “ 第三轮冲击 ” 两个阶段。
第⼆轮冲击通常规模有限，尚不⾜以引发全⾯衰退。 1997 年亚洲货币危机和 1998 年 LTCM 危
机，均发⽣在 1990 年代 IT 繁荣周期之中； 2007 年次贷危机和⻉尔斯登危机，则发⽣在 2000 年代
房地产繁荣周期之中。这两组事件均属于第⼆轮冲击，均未直接导致全⾯信⽤危机或经济衰

退。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

关键的转折点在于央⾏的政策应对。报告指出，在上述两次第⼆轮冲击发⽣时，通胀压⼒和 “ 政
策落后于曲线 ” 的担忧尚不突出，美联储因此得以迅速暂停加息并转向降息，成功将信⽤收缩扼
杀于萌芽状态，经济随之重返扩张轨道。
然⽽，在随后约⼀⾄两年的扩张之后，更⼤规模的 “ 第三轮冲击 ” 相继爆发——2000 年互联⽹泡
沫崩溃和 2008 年雷曼危机 —— 并最终引发经济衰退。报告特别指出，在这两次第三轮冲击发⽣
时，美联储均⽆法在冲击发⽣后⽴即实施货币宽松，政策被迫滞后，加剧了危机的烈度。
油价：决定央⾏政策空间的核⼼变量
Matsuzawa 将原油价格定性为衡量美联储政策灵活性的核⼼指标，也是判断当前信⽤隐忧能否
被遏制的关键所在。
逻辑链条清晰：当油价因经济过热或通胀压⼒上涨时，央⾏⾯临滞胀困境 —— 既要应对信⽤冲
击，⼜⽆法降息刺激经济，政策空间被双重挤压。这正是历史上第三轮冲击得以演变为系统性
危机的根本原因。
报告明确指出，美欧央⾏⽬前正因油价上涨⽽持续加息，市场已将美国和欧洲 12 ⽉加息的概率

定价为接近 100% ，⽇本 12 ⽉加息概率亦接近 80% 。这⼀政策取向与历史上第三轮冲击前⼣的宏
观背景⾼度吻合，构成当前最值得警惕的⻛险信号。
然⽽，报告也提供了⼀个相对乐观的情景：若油价上涨的主要驱动⼒来⾃地缘政治因素⽽⾮需
求端，则美联储仍可在信⽤冲击发⽣后保留降息空间，从⽽重演 1997 年⾄ 1998 年和 2007 年的历
史 —— 以政策转向阻断危机升级，将第⼆轮冲击控制在可管理范围之内。
当前市场信号：信⽤隐忧持续，⻛险偏好分化
报告发布当⽇，市场呈现出信⽤隐忧与⻛险偏好并存的复杂格局，印证了野村对当前处于 “ 第⼆
轮冲击 ” 阶段的判断。

⽇本国债有望受益于美欧⻓端国债上涨和油价回落。法德国债利差所反映的持续信⽤担忧，预
计将继续推动资⾦流向⻓期国债避险。与此同时，尽管油价昨⽇下跌，美欧加息预期仍⼩幅回

⽇本股市则有望受到美欧股市⾛强和油价下跌的双重⽀撑。报告指出，市场关注焦点将集中于

利差所⽰持续存在，银⾏股的修复⼒度因此受到制约。
值得注意的是，⽇本 12 ⽉加息概率已被市场定价⾄接近 80%，这意味着⽇本货币政策正逐步向
美欧靠拢，⼀旦全球信⽤⻛险进⼀步升级，⽇本央⾏的政策灵活性同样将⾯临考验。

Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

该漏洞此前已在系统中潜伏超过⼗年。⼯信部在⻛险提⽰中明确列出应对优先级：相关单
位和⽤⼾应⽴即开展全⾯排查，及时升级⾄ PHP 官⽅发布的最新安全版本。对于短期内⽆
法完成升级的机构，官⽅建议采取临时下线、关闭会话上传进度功能、⽹络隔离或访问限
制等临时防护措施，同时加强⽹络安全监测，及时更换账号、⼝令和密钥等凭证。
摘要：
⼯业和信息化部⽹络安全威胁和漏洞信息共享平台（ NVDB ）发布紧急提⽰， PHP 存在两个远程
代码执⾏超危漏洞，攻击者⽆需任何⾝份验证即可直接⼊侵⽬标系统，建议相关单位和⽤⼾⽴
即排查并升级⾄最新安全版本。
此次漏洞波及范围极⼴，全球约七成⽹站底层依赖 PHP 运⾏，从政府服务到⾦融机构均⾯临直
接安全威胁。⽹络安全公司 Cloudflare 披露，漏洞曝光后已检测到数百万次利⽤尝试；亚⻢逊则
表⽰，⿊客早在漏洞公开四天前便已着⼿尝试利⽤该缺陷。
在应对层⾯，芬兰、法国、荷兰等国率先将部分政府服务主动下线；⽇本多家航空、⾦融、消
费品及游戏企业相继宣布服务中断；阿⾥云、腾讯云亦于事件曝光后发出紧急警告，要求客⼾

尽快完成版本升级。
⽆需登录即可⼊侵，漏洞危害严重
此次被曝光的两个漏洞，核⼼⻛险在于攻击⻔槛极低。未经⾝份验证的攻击者可直接对运⾏
PHP 的服务器发动攻击，远程执⾏任意代码，进⽽导致服务器受控、敏感信息泄露及业务中
断。
受影响的 PHP 版本范围⼴泛，包括： 8.5 系列低于 8.5.12 、 8.4 系列低于 8.4.27 、 8.3 系列低于
8.3.36 、 8.2 系列低于 8.2.35 ，以及 8.1 系列及更早版本。
漏洞由美国两家⽹络安全机构 Calif 和 Wiz 的研究⼈员发现，借助 OpenAI 和⾕歌的 AI 模型辅助分
析后⽅才识别。值得关注的是，该漏洞此前已在系统中潜伏超过⼗年。

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
⼯信部在⻛险提⽰中明确列出应对优先级：相关单位和⽤⼾应⽴即开展全⾯排查，及时升级⾄
PHP 官⽅发布的最新安全版本。
对于短期内⽆法完成升级的机构，官⽅建议采取临时下线、关闭会话上传进度功能、⽹络隔离
或访问限制等临时防护措施，同时加强⽹络安全监测，及时更换账号、⼝令和密钥等凭证。
多国选择主动下线，以时间换安全
PHP 作为全球最⼴泛使⽤的服务器端脚本语⾔之⼀，为互联⽹基础设施提供底层⽀撑。⽹络安
全 公 司 W i z 估 计 ， 受 影 响 的 P H P 语 ⾔ 驱 动 了 全 球 约 7 0 % 的 ⽹ 站 ， 其 中 包 括 建 站 平 台
WordPress 。
这⼀规模意味着，从个⼈博客、中⼩企业官⽹到政府公共服务平台，均潜在处于攻击者的打击
范围之内。美国国家安全局（ NSA ）及欧洲⽹络安全机构相继就此发出警告，要求企业⽴即采

取⾏动。
⾯对漏洞曝光后攻击尝试的急速攀升，多国政府和企业选择主动关停相关服务。
在欧洲，芬兰、法国、荷兰率先对部分政府服务采取预防性下线。澳⼤利亚信号局于周三深夜
发布警告，敦促企业及⽤⼾尽快更新补丁，但仍有部分政府⽹站因此陷⼊⽆法访问状态。在亚
太地区，韩国将私营部⻔⽹络危机预警级别上调，并召集国防部、科学与信息通信技术部及国
家情报院官员举⾏紧急会议。
⽇本⽅⾯，航空公司全⽇空（ ANA ）宣布暂停⽹站及应⽤程序服务，包括 Apple Wallet 登机牌功
能；野村证券旗下⼦公司及三菱 UFJ ⾦融集团（ MUFG ）暂停部分⾮核⼼服务，三菱 UFJ 表⽰停
摆不影响⽀付业务；⽇本最⼤啤酒商朝⽇集团、铁路运营商京阪控股、游戏企业世嘉（ Sega ）
和万代南梦宫（ Bandai Namco ）亦相继宣布对相关⽹站进⾏紧急维护或暂停部分功能。
中⼩企业和普通⽤⼾同样承压
尽管此次漏洞引发⼴泛关注，部分专业⼈⼠提⽰，不同类型机构⾯临的实际⻛险存在显著差
异。
莫纳什⼤学学者 Rashina Hoda 表⽰，⼤型银⾏和企业通常不依赖 PHP 处理敏感交易，因此受到

的直接冲击相对有限。然⽽，⼤量⾼度依赖 WordPress 等建站平台的中⼩型企业，其⽹站底层
正是 PHP ，这类企业将⾯临更为迫切的修复压⼒。
Hoda 同时警告，此次漏洞曝光可能引发⽹络诈骗和⽹络钓⻥攻击的激增。攻击者往往借助重⼤
安全事件制造恐慌，诱导⽤⼾点击虚假 " 补丁链接 " 或提交账⼾信息。
